[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F12.5, F19.5, F06.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came because… well, it’s been getting hard to go into work and act like everything is normal. I drive industrial trucks at the warehouse, and the routing system there—the computer that tells us where to go, what to pick up, what scanner to use—it doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange, but it’s like it’s been marking my moves. Watching which aisles I’m in, how long I stop, when I turn around. And then the scanners, the handheld ones, they send these little messages or prompts, and it feels directed at me. Like they’re trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there, been doing it a long time, and maybe they figure I’m in the way now.\n\nI’ve tried to keep my head down, just do the routes, follow the beeps and screens, but it’s gotten to where I can’t shake the feeling that the whole system is arranged to track me and edge me out. That’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t like saying it that plain, but yes, I have.\n\nIt’s not just like remembering somebody’s voice or thinking about work too much. Sometimes I’ll be sitting out in my garage, everything shut off — no radio, no TV, the truck not running, nothing making noise — and I’ll hear what sounds like dispatch. Clear as day, like over the warehouse system, calling my operator number. Not my name always, my number, the way they do at work.\n\nAnd then it’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. That part really gets to me, because I’ll look around and I know nobody’s standing there in the garage, nobody’s outside that I can see, but it sounds like it’s coming through anyway. It’s… it’s not muffled or like a dream. It sounds real enough that I’ll stop what I’m doing and listen.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine, amphetamines, cocaine, or any other street drugs recently?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not using things like that. I know people probably wonder when somebody says what I’m saying, but I’m not out there doing meth or coke or anything like that.\n\nI’ve never been the type to mess with street drugs, especially with the kind of work I do. You’re operating heavy equipment, moving around pallets and racks and all that—if you’re not clearheaded, somebody can get hurt. So no, not recently. Not that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a head injury or seizure recently?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I’ve bumped my head here and there over the years, working around equipment and all that, but nothing recently where I blacked out or had to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nAnd seizures… no, I don’t think so. Nobody’s told me I’ve had one, and I haven’t had an episode where I woke up on the floor or lost time in that way. If you mean something more subtle, I guess you’d have to tell me what to look for, but as far as I know, no head injury or seizure recently.\n\nDoctor: How long have these voices been happening?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I want to admit, honestly. The voices, and the thing with the scanners — that’s been going on for about a year and eight months now, give or take.\n\nAt first I kept telling myself it was just stress or noise carrying over from work, because when you hear dispatch and numbers all day, your mind can kind of keep replaying things. But it didn’t really go away. It became this regular thing, and after a while I couldn’t keep pretending it was just a bad day or being tired.\n\nSo, yeah… about a year and eight months. Not just a couple weeks. It’s been hanging around a long time.\n\nDoctor: Were you taking any prescription stimulant, like Adderall or Ritalin, when the voices first began?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I remember. I mean, I know Adderall and Ritalin are those focus medicines, right? I’ve heard of them, but I don’t recall being on anything like that when this started.\n\nI’m trying to think back because it’s been a while, and back then I was still telling myself it was just stress or work noise stuck in my head, so I wasn’t exactly keeping notes. But no, I don’t remember taking a prescription stimulant or anything in that category when the voices first began.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a period of at least a few days when you felt unusually high, energized, or like you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it “high,” no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m wound up, where I can’t settle down very well, but that feels more like being stressed or bothered than feeling good or energized.\n\nAs far as needing much less sleep… I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I was sleeping only a couple hours and felt fine or anything like that. When I don’t sleep well, I usually feel it. I’m tired, dragging, irritable maybe, but not like I’m full of energy and ready to take on the world.\n\nSo… I don’t think so. Not a few days in a row where I felt unusually high or didn’t need sleep. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: During the time the voices were happening, was there at least one full month when you were not using any drugs at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’d have to think back pretty carefully, because I don’t really keep track of time like that in my head, especially when everything was already kind of blurred together.\n\nWhen you say “drugs,” do you mean like anything illegal, or are you including prescriptions, alcohol, over-the-counter stuff too? Because I don’t want to answer wrong and make it sound cleaner or worse than it was.\n\nI can say I don’t remember the voices being tied neatly to any one thing I was using or not using. It wasn’t like, “Oh, that only happened during this stretch,” or “it stopped when I wasn’t taking anything.” But whether there was one full month with absolutely no drugs at all… I honestly don’t know. I’d need to go back and think through it more carefully.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when you were completely off alcohol, marijuana, and any non-prescribed drugs?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know you’re narrowing it down more now — alcohol, marijuana, and anything non-prescribed — and that helps, but I still don’t want to give you a clean “yes” or “no” if I can’t really back it up.\n\nThere may have been stretches where I wasn’t using anything like that, but a full month… I’d have to think hard about it. My memory of that period isn’t organized neatly by weeks and months. It’s more like I remember certain days or rough patches, not a clear calendar of what I did or didn’t use.\n\nSo the most honest answer is: I don’t know for sure. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. I know you’re narrowing it down more now — alcohol, marijuana, and anything non-prescribed — and that helps, but I still don’t want to give you a clean “yes” or “no” if I can’t really back it up.\n\nThere may have been stretches where I wasn’t using anything like that, but a full month… I’d have to think hard about it. My memory of that period isn’t organized neatly by weeks and months. It’s more like I remember certain days or rough patches, not a clear calendar of what I did or didn’t use.\n\nSo the most honest answer is: I don’t know for sure. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. But my wife has said it to me more than once, that I’m not really “there” the way I used to be.\n\nShe says my face has gone kind of flat, like I’m not reacting to things. And she’ll talk to me and I’ll just… barely answer. Not because I’m trying to be rude to her. I hear her, I just can’t seem to get much out, or I don’t have the energy to make conversation go anywhere.\n\nMost days, honestly, I’ll sit at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee and just stare at it. I’ll know there are things that need doing — normal things around the house, calls to make, chores, whatever — and I just don’t start. I just sit there. And then the day kind of goes by.\n\nSo yes, being around people, talking, doing things… it’s all less than it used to be. I’ve pulled back a lot.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these voices first started?\n\nPatient: I was… let me think. I’m 62 now, so I would’ve been about 60 when it really started. Maybe a little past 60.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a year and eight months, the voices and the thing with the scanners feeling like they were sending messages or tracking me. It wasn’t like one clean day where I can say, “That was the exact start,” because at first I tried to brush it off or explain it as work stress or equipment acting strange. But looking back, that’s about how long it’s been — close to two years, but not quite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any neurological illness, such as Parkinson’s disease or dementia?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. But my wife has said it to me more than once, that I’m not really “there” the way I used to be.\n\nShe says my face has gone kind of flat, like I’m not reacting to things. And she’ll talk to me and I’ll just… barely answer. Not because I’m trying to be rude to her. I hear her, I just can’t seem to get much out, or I don’t have the energy to make conversation go anywhere.\n\nMost days, honestly, I’ll sit at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee and just stare at it. I’ll know there are things that need doing — normal things around the house, calls to make, chores, whatever — and I just don’t start. I just sit there. And then the day kind of goes by.\n\nSo yes, being around people, talking, doing things… it’s all less than it used to be. I’ve pulled back a lot.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these voices first started?\n\nPatient: I was… let me think. I’m 62 now, so I would’ve been about 60 when it really started. Maybe a little past 60.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a year and eight months, the voices and the thing with the scanners feeling like they were sending messages or tracking me. It wasn’t like one clean day where I can say, “That was the exact start,” because at first I tried to brush it off or explain it as work stress or equipment acting strange. But looking back, that’s about how long it’s been — close to two years, but not quite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any neurological illness, such as Parkinson’s disease or dementia?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. Nobody’s told me I have Parkinson’s or dementia or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual worries because I’m 62 now, so when something feels off, your mind does go there a little bit — like, “Is this something with my brain?” But as far as an actual diagnosis from a doctor, no. I haven’t been diagnosed with a neurological illness like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… no, not really seeing, like with my eyes. It’s more hearing. That’s the part that scares me.\n\nEven if I’m sitting in my garage, with everything shut off — no radio, no scanner, no TV, nothing running — I can still hear dispatch voices clear as day. Like they’re right there, calling my operator number. And they’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me.\n\nI know you asked about seeing things, and I’m trying to answer that straight. I don’t think I’m seeing people standing there or objects that aren’t there. But the voices feel very real when it happens. It’s not like a thought in my head exactly. It sounds like it’s coming from outside, like a real dispatch call.\n\nDoctor: Was there a period of at least one month when you were completely off marijuana?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that, because I don’t really have a clean timeline in my head for it.\n\nWhen you say “completely off,” do you mean not using it at all — like zero — for a full month straight? I’d have to think hard about that. I don’t want to tell you yes or no and be wrong. My memory for exact stretches like that isn’t great unless I had written it down, and I didn’t. There may have been times I wasn’t using any, or wasn’t around it, but whether it was a full month, completely… I’m just not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. There may have been times I wasn’t using any, or wasn’t around it, but whether it was a full month, completely… I’m just not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having problems with your memory?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Nobody’s told me I have Parkinson’s or dementia or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual worries because I’m 62 now, so when something feels off, your mind does go there a little bit — like, “Is this something with my brain?” But as far as an actual diagnosis from a doctor, no. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Memory… I mean, I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve got some clear memory problem, like I’m forgetting where I live or not recognizing people. Nothing like that.

But I do get mixed up on timelines. Like when you asked me about whether I was off marijuana for a full month, that’s the kind of thing I have trouble answering, because unless I wrote it down, I can’t always pin down exactly when something happened or how long it lasted. Some days sort of run together, especially if I’m stressed or not sleeping well.

I misplace little things sometimes, but I think a lot of people do that. Keys, papers, my phone — then I’ll find them and feel stupid. I don’t know if that counts as memory trouble or just being distracted. I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a major memory problem, but I’m not always confident about dates and details.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F12.5, F19.5, F06.2, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came because… well, it’s been getting hard to go into work and act like everything is normal. I drive industrial trucks at the warehouse, and the routing system there—the computer that tells us where to go, what to pick up, what scanner to use—it doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange, but it’s like it’s been marking my moves. Watching which aisles I’m in, how long I stop, when I turn around. And then the scanners, the handheld ones, they send these little messages or prompts, and it feels directed at me. Like they’re trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there, been doing it a long time, and maybe they figure I’m in the way now.\n\nI’ve tried to keep my head down, just do the routes, follow the beeps and screens, but it’s gotten to where I can’t shake the feeling that the whole system is arranged to track me and edge me out. That’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t like saying it that plain, but yes, I have.\n\nIt’s not just like remembering somebody’s voice or thinking about work too much. Sometimes I’ll be sitting out in my garage, everything shut off — no radio, no TV, the truck not running, nothing making noise — and I’ll hear what sounds like dispatch. Clear as day, like over the warehouse system, calling my operator number. Not my name always, my number, the way they do at work.\n\nAnd then it’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. That part really gets to me, because I’ll look around and I know nobody’s standing there in the garage, nobody’s outside that I can see, but it sounds like it’s coming through anyway. It’s… it’s not muffled or like a dream. It sounds real enough that I’ll stop what I’m doing and listen.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine, amphetamines, cocaine, or any other street drugs recently?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not using things like that. I know people probably wonder when somebody says what I’m saying, but I’m not out there doing meth or coke or anything like that.\n\nI’ve never been the type to mess with street drugs, especially with the kind of work I do. You’re operating heavy equipment, moving around pallets and racks and all that—if you’re not clearheaded, somebody can get hurt. So no, not recently. Not that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a head injury or seizure recently?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I’ve bumped my head here and there over the years, working around equipment and all that, but nothing recently where I blacked out or had to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nAnd seizures… no, I don’t think so. Nobody’s told me I’ve had one, and I haven’t had an episode where I woke up on the floor or lost time in that way. If you mean something more subtle, I guess you’d have to tell me what to look for, but as far as I know, no head injury or seizure recently.\n\nDoctor: How long have these voices been happening?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than I want to admit, honestly. The voices, and the thing with the scanners — that’s been going on for about a year and eight months now, give or take.\n\nAt first I kept telling myself it was just stress or noise carrying over from work, because when you hear dispatch and numbers all day, your mind can kind of keep replaying things. But it didn’t really go away. It became this regular thing, and after a while I couldn’t keep pretending it was just a bad day or being tired.\n\nSo, yeah… about a year and eight months. Not just a couple weeks. It’s been hanging around a long time.\n\nDoctor: Were you taking any prescription stimulant, like Adderall or Ritalin, when the voices first began?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I remember. I mean, I know Adderall and Ritalin are those focus medicines, right? I’ve heard of them, but I don’t recall being on anything like that when this started.\n\nI’m trying to think back because it’s been a while, and back then I was still telling myself it was just stress or work noise stuck in my head, so I wasn’t exactly keeping notes. But no, I don’t remember taking a prescription stimulant or anything in that category when the voices first began.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a period of at least a few days when you felt unusually high, energized, or like you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it “high,” no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m wound up, where I can’t settle down very well, but that feels more like being stressed or bothered than feeling good or energized.\n\nAs far as needing much less sleep… I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I was sleeping only a couple hours and felt fine or anything like that. When I don’t sleep well, I usually feel it. I’m tired, dragging, irritable maybe, but not like I’m full of energy and ready to take on the world.\n\nSo… I don’t think so. Not a few days in a row where I felt unusually high or didn’t need sleep. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: During the time the voices were happening, was there at least one full month when you were not using any drugs at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’d have to think back pretty carefully, because I don’t really keep track of time like that in my head, especially when everything was already kind of blurred together.\n\nWhen you say “drugs,” do you mean like anything illegal, or are you including prescriptions, alcohol, over-the-counter stuff too? Because I don’t want to answer wrong and make it sound cleaner or worse than it was.\n\nI can say I don’t remember the voices being tied neatly to any one thing I was using or not using. It wasn’t like, “Oh, that only happened during this stretch,” or “it stopped when I wasn’t taking anything.” But whether there was one full month with absolutely no drugs at all… I honestly don’t know. I’d need to go back and think through it more carefully.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when you were completely off alcohol, marijuana, and any non-prescribed drugs?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know you’re narrowing it down more now — alcohol, marijuana, and anything non-prescribed — and that helps, but I still don’t want to give you a clean “yes” or “no” if I can’t really back it up.\n\nThere may have been stretches where I wasn’t using anything like that, but a full month… I’d have to think hard about it. My memory of that period isn’t organized neatly by weeks and months. It’s more like I remember certain days or rough patches, not a clear calendar of what I did or didn’t use.\n\nSo the most honest answer is: I don’t know for sure. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. But my wife has said it to me more than once, that I’m not really “there” the way I used to be.\n\nShe says my face has gone kind of flat, like I’m not reacting to things. And she’ll talk to me and I’ll just… barely answer. Not because I’m trying to be rude to her. I hear her, I just can’t seem to get much out, or I don’t have the energy to make conversation go anywhere.\n\nMost days, honestly, I’ll sit at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee and just stare at it. I’ll know there are things that need doing — normal things around the house, calls to make, chores, whatever — and I just don’t start. I just sit there. And then the day kind of goes by.\n\nSo yes, being around people, talking, doing things… it’s all less than it used to be. I’ve pulled back a lot.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these voices first started?\n\nPatient: I was… let me think. I’m 62 now, so I would’ve been about 60 when it really started. Maybe a little past 60.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a year and eight months, the voices and the thing with the scanners feeling like they were sending messages or tracking me. It wasn’t like one clean day where I can say, “That was the exact start,” because at first I tried to brush it off or explain it as work stress or equipment acting strange. But looking back, that’s about how long it’s been — close to two years, but not quite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any neurological illness, such as Parkinson’s disease or dementia?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. Nobody’s told me I have Parkinson’s or dementia or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual worries because I’m 62 now, so when something feels off, your mind does go there a little bit — like, “Is this something with my brain?” But as far as an actual diagnosis from a doctor, no. I haven’t been diagnosed with a neurological illness like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… no, not really seeing, like with my eyes. It’s more hearing. That’s the part that scares me.\n\nEven if I’m sitting in my garage, with everything shut off — no radio, no scanner, no TV, nothing running — I can still hear dispatch voices clear as day. Like they’re right there, calling my operator number. I’d need to go back and think through it more carefully.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when you were completely off alcohol, marijuana, and any non-prescribed drugs?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know you’re narrowing it down more now — alcohol, marijuana, and anything non-prescribed — and that helps, but I still don’t want to give you a clean “yes” or “no” if I can’t really back it up.\n\nThere may have been stretches where I wasn’t using anything like that, but a full month… I’d have to think hard about it. My memory of that period isn’t organized neatly by weeks and months. It’s more like I remember certain days or rough patches, not a clear calendar of what I did or didn’t use.\n\nSo the most honest answer is: I don’t know for sure. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. But my wife has said it to me more than once, that I’m not really “there” the way I used to be.\n\nShe says my face has gone kind of flat, like I’m not reacting to things. And she’ll talk to me and I’ll just… barely answer. Not because I’m trying to be rude to her. I hear her, I just can’t seem to get much out, or I don’t have the energy to make conversation go anywhere.\n\nMost days, honestly, I’ll sit at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee and just stare at it. I’ll know there are things that need doing — normal things around the house, calls to make, chores, whatever — and I just don’t start. I just sit there. And then the day kind of goes by.\n\nSo yes, being around people, talking, doing things… it’s all less than it used to be. I’ve pulled back a lot.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these voices first started?\n\nPatient: I was… let me think. I’m 62 now, so I would’ve been about 60 when it really started. Maybe a little past 60.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a year and eight months, the voices and the thing with the scanners feeling like they were sending messages or tracking me. It wasn’t like one clean day where I can say, “That was the exact start,” because at first I tried to brush it off or explain it as work stress or equipment acting strange. But looking back, that’s about how long it’s been — close to two years, but not quite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any neurological illness, such as Parkinson’s disease or dementia?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. Nobody’s told me I have Parkinson’s or dementia or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual worries because I’m 62 now, so when something feels off, your mind does go there a little bit — like, “Is this something with my brain?” But as far as an actual diagnosis from a doctor, no. I haven’t been diagnosed with a neurological illness like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… no, not really seeing, like with my eyes. It’s more hearing. That’s the part that scares me.\n\nEven if I’m sitting in my garage, with everything shut off — no radio, no scanner, no TV, nothing running — I can still hear dispatch voices clear as day. Like they’re right there, calling my operator number. And they’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me.\n\nI know you asked about seeing things, and I’m trying to answer that straight. I don’t think I’m seeing people standing there or objects that aren’t there. But the voices feel very real when it happens. It’s not like a thought in my head exactly. It sounds like it’s coming from outside, like a real dispatch call.\n\nDoctor: Was there a period of at least one month when you were completely off marijuana?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that, because I don’t really have a clean timeline in my head for it.\n\nWhen you say “completely off,” do you mean not using it at all — like zero — for a full month straight? I’d have to think hard about that. I don’t want to tell you yes or no and be wrong. My memory for exact stretches like that isn’t great unless I had written it down, and I didn’t. There may have been times I wasn’t using any, or wasn’t around it, but whether it was a full month, completely… I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having problems with your memory?\n\nPatient: Memory… I mean, I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve got some clear memory problem, like I’m forgetting where I live or not recognizing people. Nothing like that.\n\nBut I do get mixed up on timelines. Like when you asked me about whether I was off marijuana for a full month, that’s the kind of thing I have trouble answering, because unless I wrote it down, I can’t always pin down exactly when something happened or how long it lasted. Some days sort of run together, especially if I’m stressed or not sleeping well.\n\nI misplace little things sometimes, but I think a lot of people do that. Keys, papers, my phone — then I’ll find them and feel stupid. I don’t know if that counts as memory trouble or just being distracted. Getting lost… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of being somewhere I’ve known for years and suddenly not knowing how to get home or anything like that.

I mean, I can get turned around if I’m distracted, or if I’m in some big medical building with too many hallways and signs, but that’s always been a little annoying for me. I’ve heard of them, but I don’t recall being on anything like that when this started.\n\nI’m trying to think back because it’s been a while, and back then I was still telling myself it was just stress or work noise stuck in my head, so I wasn’t exactly keeping notes. But no, I don’t remember taking a prescription stimulant or anything in that category when the voices first began.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a period of at least a few days when you felt unusually high, energized, or like you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it “high,” no. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m wound up, where I can’t settle down very well, but that feels more like being stressed or bothered than feeling good or energized.\n\nAs far as needing much less sleep… I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I was sleeping only a couple hours and felt fine or anything like that. When I don’t sleep well, I usually feel it. I’m tired, dragging, irritable maybe, but not like I’m full of energy and ready to take on the world.\n\nSo… I don’t think so. Not a few days in a row where I felt unusually high or didn’t need sleep. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: During the time the voices were happening, was there at least one full month when you were not using any drugs at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’d have to think back pretty carefully, because I don’t really keep track of time like that in my head, especially when everything was already kind of blurred together.\n\nWhen you say “drugs,” do you mean like anything illegal, or are you including prescriptions, alcohol, over-the-counter stuff too? Because I don’t want to answer wrong and make it sound cleaner or worse than it was.\n\nI can say I don’t remember the voices being tied neatly to any one thing I was using or not using. It wasn’t like, “Oh, that only happened during this stretch,” or “it stopped when I wasn’t taking anything.” But whether there was one full month with absolutely no drugs at all… I honestly don’t know. I’d need to go back and think through it more carefully.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a full month when you were completely off alcohol, marijuana, and any non-prescribed drugs?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know you’re narrowing it down more now — alcohol, marijuana, and anything non-prescribed — and that helps, but I still don’t want to give you a clean “yes” or “no” if I can’t really back it up.\n\nThere may have been stretches where I wasn’t using anything like that, but a full month… I’d have to think hard about it. My memory of that period isn’t organized neatly by weeks and months. It’s more like I remember certain days or rough patches, not a clear calendar of what I did or didn’t use.\n\nSo the most honest answer is: I don’t know for sure. I can’t confidently say there was a full month where I was completely off all of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using marijuana regularly during the time these voices have been happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “regularly,” but I’m also not sure I’m the best judge of that, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t want to sit here and say, “No, not regular,” and then if you looked at it from the outside you’d think, well, that actually was pretty regular.\n\nThere have been times I used marijuana, yes. But during the whole time the voices were happening, I can’t lay it out cleanly and say it was every day, or every week, or only once in a while. My memory around that is kind of patchy and not organized in a way where I can give you a reliable schedule.\n\nI guess the honest answer is: I don’t know. I can’t confidently say I was using it regularly, but I also can’t confidently say I wasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like they can hear every word in my head, no… but I do feel like things I’m thinking or doing are being tracked, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I’m here. At the warehouse, the routing system — the one that sends jobs through the scanners and tells you where to go — it doesn’t feel like just a computer system anymore. I know how that probably sounds, but I’ve worked around those systems a long time, and I know when something is off. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, what I do, how fast I do it, and then sending these little messages through the scanner assignments, like nudges or warnings.\n\nIt’s not like a person standing there saying, “I can read your mind.” It’s more like the system knows too much. Like it knows when I’m hesitating or when I’m questioning what’s happening, and then the next route or scan will come through in a way that feels aimed at me. And I keep thinking it’s to push me out because I’m one of the older guys there, one of the old hands, and maybe they don’t want me around anymore.\n\nSo, thoughts being read… I don’t know if I’d put it exactly that way. But being watched and singled out through the scanners? Yes. That feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever happened when you were completely sober?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, that’s happened when I was completely sober.\n\nThere are times I’ll be at home, just sitting out in my garage, and there’s nothing on — no radio, no TV, no work scanner, no machinery noise, nothing like that. Everything is shut off. And I’ll still hear what sounds like dispatch voices, clear as day, like they’re calling my operator number. Not muffled, not like I’m half-asleep dreaming it. It sounds directed at me.\n\nThey’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me. And that’s what scares me, because I can’t explain it away as, “Oh, that was the scanner,” or “That was somebody outside,” because I check. I sit there and listen, and the garage is quiet, and it still happens.\n\nSo… yes. It hasn’t only happened when I’d been drinking or using anything. It’s happened when I was sober, just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t know if I noticed it right away myself, because when you’re in it, it just feels like you’re tired or you don’t have it in you. But my wife has said it to me more than once, that I’m not really “there” the way I used to be.\n\nShe says my face has gone kind of flat, like I’m not reacting to things. And she’ll talk to me and I’ll just… barely answer. Not because I’m trying to be rude to her. I hear her, I just can’t seem to get much out, or I don’t have the energy to make conversation go anywhere.\n\nMost days, honestly, I’ll sit at the kitchen table with a cup of coffee and just stare at it. I’ll know there are things that need doing — normal things around the house, calls to make, chores, whatever — and I just don’t start. I just sit there. And then the day kind of goes by.\n\nSo yes, being around people, talking, doing things… it’s all less than it used to be. I’ve pulled back a lot.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these voices first started?\n\nPatient: I was… let me think. I’m 62 now, so I would’ve been about 60 when it really started. Maybe a little past 60.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a year and eight months, the voices and the thing with the scanners feeling like they were sending messages or tracking me. It wasn’t like one clean day where I can say, “That was the exact start,” because at first I tried to brush it off or explain it as work stress or equipment acting strange. But looking back, that’s about how long it’s been — close to two years, but not quite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any neurological illness, such as Parkinson’s disease or dementia?\n\nPatient: No… not that I know of. Nobody’s told me I have Parkinson’s or dementia or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual worries because I’m 62 now, so when something feels off, your mind does go there a little bit — like, “Is this something with my brain?” But as far as an actual diagnosis from a doctor, no. I haven’t been diagnosed with a neurological illness like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things… no, not really seeing, like with my eyes. It’s more hearing. That’s the part that scares me.\n\nEven if I’m sitting in my garage, with everything shut off — no radio, no scanner, no TV, nothing running — I can still hear dispatch voices clear as day. Like they’re right there, calling my operator number. And they’ll say things like I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me.\n\nI know you asked about seeing things, and I’m trying to answer that straight. I don’t think I’m seeing people standing there or objects that aren’t there. But the voices feel very real when it happens. It’s not like a thought in my head exactly. It sounds like it’s coming from outside, like a real dispatch call.\n\nDoctor: Was there a period of at least one month when you were completely off marijuana?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that, because I don’t really have a clean timeline in my head for it.\n\nWhen you say “completely off,” do you mean not using it at all — like zero — for a full month straight? I’d have to think hard about that. I don’t want to tell you yes or no and be wrong. My memory for exact stretches like that isn’t great unless I had written it down, and I didn’t. There may have been times I wasn’t using any, or wasn’t around it, but whether it was a full month, completely… I’m just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having problems with your memory?\n\nPatient: Memory… I mean, I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve got some clear memory problem, like I’m forgetting where I live or not recognizing people. Nothing like that.\n\nBut I do get mixed up on timelines. Like when you asked me about whether I was off marijuana for a full month, that’s the kind of thing I have trouble answering, because unless I wrote it down, I can’t always pin down exactly when something happened or how long it lasted. Some days sort of run together, especially if I’m stressed or not sleeping well.\n\nI misplace little things sometimes, but I think a lot of people do that. Keys, papers, my phone — then I’ll find them and feel stupid. I don’t know if that counts as memory trouble or just being distracted. I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a major memory problem, but I’m not always confident about dates and details.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting lost in places that used to be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: Getting lost… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of being somewhere I’ve known for years and suddenly not knowing how to get home or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get turned around if I’m distracted, or if I’m in some big medical building with too many hallways and signs, but that’s always been a little annoying for me. Around my usual places — home, the store, roads I know, places I’ve worked around — I haven’t noticed myself getting lost like they’re unfamiliar.\n\nSometimes I’ll take a second and think, “Wait, which way was I going?” but then it comes back. So I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem, no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },
